c7:T562,<p>The <dfn>Number prototype object</dfn>:</p>
      <ul>
        <li>is <dfn><emu-intrinsic>%Number.prototype%</emu-intrinsic></dfn>.</li>
        <li>is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary object</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        <li>is itself a Number object; it has a <var class="field">[[NumberData]]</var> internal slot with the value <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li>
        <li>has a <var class="field">[[Prototype]]</var> internal slot whose value is <emu-intrinsic>%Object.prototype%</emu-intrinsic>.</li>
      </ul>
      <p>Unless explicitly stated otherwise, the methods of the Number prototype object defined below are not generic and the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value passed to them must be either a Number value or an object that has a <var class="field">[[NumberData]]</var> internal slot that has been initialized to a Number value.</p>
      <p>The phrase “this Number value” within the specification of a method refers to the result returned by calling the abstract operation <emu-xref aoid="ThisNumberValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-thisnumbervalue">ThisNumberValue</a></emu-xref> with the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value of the method invocation passed as the argument.</p>

      c8:T2148,<p>This method returns a String containing this Number value represented in decimal exponential notation with one digit before the significand's decimal point and <var>fractionDigits</var> digits after the significand's decimal point. If <var>fractionDigits</var> is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, it includes as many significand digits as necessary to uniquely specify the Number (just like in <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref> except that in this case the Number is always output in exponential notation).</p>
        <p>It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>number</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ThisNumberValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-thisnumbervalue">ThisNumberValue</a></emu-xref>(<emu-val>this</emu-val> value).</li><li>Let <var>fractionCount</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToIntegerOrInfinity"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tointegerorinfinity">ToIntegerOrInfinity</a></emu-xref>(<var>fractionDigits</var>).</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: If <var>fractionDigits</var> is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then <var>fractionCount</var> is 0.</li><li class="exit">If <var>number</var> is not <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref>, return <emu-xref aoid="Number::toString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-numeric-types-number-tostring">Number::toString</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>, 10).</li><li class="exit">If <var>fractionCount</var> &lt; 0 or <var>fractionCount</var> > 100, throw a <emu-val>RangeError</emu-val> exception.</li><li>Set <var>number</var> to <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>).</li><li>Let <var>sign</var> be the empty String.</li><li>If <var>number</var> &lt; 0, then<ol><li>Set <var>sign</var> to <emu-val>"-"</emu-val>.</li><li>Set <var>number</var> to -<var>number</var>.</li></ol></li><li>If <var>number</var> = 0, then<ol><li>Let <var>significand</var> be the String value consisting of <var>fractionCount</var> + 1 occurrences of the code unit 0x0030 (DIGIT ZERO).</li><li>Let <var>exponent</var> be 0.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li>If <var>fractionDigits</var> is not <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>exponent</var> and <var>intSignificand</var> be <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> such that 10<sup><var>fractionCount</var></sup> ≤ <var>intSignificand</var> &lt; 10<sup><var>fractionCount</var> + 1</sup> and for which <var>intSignificand</var> × 10<sup><var>exponent</var> - <var>fractionCount</var></sup> - <var>number</var> is as close to zero as possible. If there are two such sets of <var>exponent</var> and <var>intSignificand</var>, pick the <var>exponent</var> and <var>intSignificand</var> for which <var>intSignificand</var> × 10<sup><var>exponent</var> - <var>fractionCount</var></sup> is larger.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li id="step-number-proto-toexponential-intermediate-values">Let <var>exponent</var>, <var>intSignificand</var>, and <var>ff</var> be <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> such that <var>ff</var> ≥ 0, 10<sup><var>ff</var></sup> ≤ <var>intSignificand</var> &lt; 10<sup><var>ff</var> + 1</sup>, <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>intSignificand</var> × 10<sup><var>exponent</var> - <var>ff</var></sup>) is <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>), and <var>ff</var> is as small as possible. Note that the decimal representation of <var>intSignificand</var> has <var>ff</var> + 1 digits, <var>intSignificand</var> is not divisible by 10, and the least significant digit of <var>intSignificand</var> is not necessarily uniquely determined by these criteria.</li><li>Set <var>fractionCount</var> to <var>ff</var>.</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>significand</var> be the String value consisting of the digits of the decimal representation of <var>intSignificand</var> (in order, with no leading zeroes).</li></ol></li><li>If <var>fractionCount</var> ≠ 0, then<ol><li>Let <var>intPart</var> be the first code unit of <var>significand</var>.</li><li>Let <var>fractionalPart</var> be the other <var>fractionCount</var> code units of <var>significand</var>.</li><li>Set <var>significand</var> to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation">string-concatenation</a></emu-xref> of <var>intPart</var>, <emu-val>"."</emu-val>, and <var>fractionalPart</var>.</li></ol></li><li>If <var>exponent</var> = 0, then<ol><li>Let <var>exponentSign</var> be <emu-val>"+"</emu-val>.</li><li>Let <var>exponentDigits</var> be <emu-val>"0"</emu-val>.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li>If <var>exponent</var> > 0, then<ol><li>Let <var>exponentSign</var> be <emu-val>"+"</emu-val>.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>exponent</var> &lt; 0.</li><li>Let <var>exponentSign</var> be <emu-val>"-"</emu-val>.</li><li>Set <var>exponent</var> to -<var>exponent</var>.</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>exponentDigits</var> be the String value consisting of the digits of the decimal representation of <var>exponent</var> (in order, with no leading zeroes).</li></ol></li><li>Set <var>significand</var> to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation">string-concatenation</a></emu-xref> of <var>significand</var>, <emu-val>"e"</emu-val>, <var>exponentSign</var>, and <var>exponentDigits</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation">string-concatenation</a></emu-xref> of <var>sign</var> and <var>significand</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>For implementations that provide more accurate conversions than required by the rules above, it is recommended that the following alternative version of step <emu-xref href="#step-number-proto-toexponential-intermediate-values"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#step-number-proto-toexponential-intermediate-values">10.b.i</a></emu-xref> be used as a guideline:</p>
          <emu-alg replaces-step="step-number-proto-toexponential-intermediate-values"><ol><li>Let <var>exponent</var>, <var>intSignificand</var>, and <var>ff</var> be <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> such that <var>ff</var> ≥ 0, 10<sup><var>ff</var></sup> ≤ <var>intSignificand</var> &lt; 10<sup><var>ff</var> + 1</sup>, <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>intSignificand</var> × 10<sup><var>exponent</var> - <var>ff</var></sup>) is <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>), and <var>ff</var> is as small as possible. If there are multiple possibilities for <var>intSignificand</var>, choose <var>intSignificand</var> such that <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>intSignificand</var> × 10<sup><var>exponent</var> - <var>ff</var></sup>) is closest to <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>). If there are two such possible values of <var>intSignificand</var>, choose the one that is even.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        </div></emu-note>

de:T1171,<p>Time measurement in ECMAScript is analogous to time measurement in POSIX, in particular sharing definition in terms of the proleptic Gregorian calendar, an <dfn id="epoch">epoch</dfn> of midnight at the beginning of 1 January 1970 UTC, and an accounting of every day as comprising exactly 86,400 seconds (each of which is 1000 milliseconds long).</p>
        <p>An ECMAScript <dfn variants="time values">time value</dfn> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-number-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-number-type">is a Number</a></emu-xref>, either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref> representing an instant in time to millisecond precision or <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> representing no specific instant. A time value that is a multiple of <emu-eqn class="inline">24 × 60 × 60 × 1000 = 86,400,000</emu-eqn> (i.e., is 86,400,000 × <var>d</var> for some <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> <var>d</var>) represents the instant at the start of the UTC day that follows the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch">epoch</a></emu-xref> by <var>d</var> whole UTC days (preceding the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch">epoch</a></emu-xref> for negative <var>d</var>). Every other <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> time value <var>tv</var> is defined relative to the greatest preceding time value <var>s</var> that is such a multiple, and represents the instant that occurs within the same UTC day as <var>s</var> but follows it by (<var>tv</var> - <var>s</var>) milliseconds.</p>
        <p>Time values do not account for UTC leap seconds—there are no time values representing instants within positive leap seconds, and there are time values representing instants removed from the UTC timeline by negative leap seconds. However, the definition of time values nonetheless yields piecewise alignment with UTC, with discontinuities only at leap second boundaries and zero difference outside of leap seconds.</p>
        <p>A Number can exactly represent all <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> from -9,007,199,254,740,992 to 9,007,199,254,740,992 (<emu-xref href="#sec-number.min_safe_integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-number.min_safe_integer">21.1.2.8</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref href="#sec-number.max_safe_integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-number.max_safe_integer">21.1.2.6</a></emu-xref>). A time value supports a slightly smaller range of -8,640,000,000,000,000 to 8,640,000,000,000,000 milliseconds. This yields a supported time value range of exactly -100,000,000 days to 100,000,000 days relative to midnight at the beginning of 1 January 1970 UTC.</p>
        <p>The exact moment of midnight at the beginning of 1 January 1970 UTC is represented by the time value <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</p>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>In the proleptic Gregorian calendar, leap years are precisely those which are both divisible by 4 and either divisible by 400 or not divisible by 100.</p>
          <p>The 400 year cycle of the proleptic Gregorian calendar contains 97 leap years. This yields an average of 365.2425 days per year, which is 31,556,952,000 milliseconds. Therefore, the maximum range a Number could represent exactly with millisecond precision is approximately -285,426 to 285,426 years relative to 1970. The smaller range supported by a time value as specified in this section is approximately -273,790 to 273,790 years relative to 1970.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

107:T15b8,<emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This method returns a String containing this Number value represented in decimal fixed-point notation with <var>fractionDigits</var> digits after the decimal point. If <var>fractionDigits</var> is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, 0 is assumed.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <p>It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>number</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ThisNumberValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-thisnumbervalue">ThisNumberValue</a></emu-xref>(<emu-val>this</emu-val> value).</li><li>Let <var>fractionCount</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToIntegerOrInfinity"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tointegerorinfinity">ToIntegerOrInfinity</a></emu-xref>(<var>fractionDigits</var>).</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: If <var>fractionDigits</var> is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then <var>fractionCount</var> is 0.</li><li class="exit">If <var>fractionCount</var> is not <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref>, throw a <emu-val>RangeError</emu-val> exception.</li><li class="exit">If <var>fractionCount</var> &lt; 0 or <var>fractionCount</var> > 100, throw a <emu-val>RangeError</emu-val> exception.</li><li class="exit">If <var>number</var> is not <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref>, return <emu-xref aoid="Number::toString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-numeric-types-number-tostring">Number::toString</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>, 10).</li><li>Set <var>number</var> to <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>).</li><li>Let <var>sign</var> be the empty String.</li><li>If <var>number</var> &lt; 0, then<ol><li>Set <var>sign</var> to <emu-val>"-"</emu-val>.</li><li>Set <var>number</var> to -<var>number</var>.</li></ol></li><li>If <var>number</var> ≥ 10<sup>21</sup>, then<ol><li>Let <var>digitString</var> be ! 10f:T4d5,<p>The <dfn>BigInt prototype object</dfn>:</p>
      <ul>
        <li>is <dfn><emu-intrinsic>%BigInt.prototype%</emu-intrinsic></dfn>.</li>
        <li>is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary object</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        <li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-bigint-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-bigint-type">is not a BigInt</a></emu-xref> object; it does not have a <var class="field">[[BigIntData]]</var> internal slot.</li>
        <li>has a <var class="field">[[Prototype]]</var> internal slot whose value is <emu-intrinsic>%Object.prototype%</emu-intrinsic>.</li>
      </ul>
      <p>The phrase “this BigInt value” within the specification of a method refers to the result returned by calling the abstract operation <emu-xref aoid="ThisBigIntValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-thisbigintvalue">ThisBigIntValue</a></emu-xref> with the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value of the method invocation passed as the argument.</p>

      110:T445,<p>An ECMAScript implementation that includes the ECMA-402 Internationalization API must implement this method as specified in the ECMA-402 specification. If an ECMAScript implementation does not include the ECMA-402 API the following specification of this method is used:</p>
        <p>This method produces a String value that represents this BigInt value formatted according to the conventions of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref>'s current locale. This method is <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref>, and it is permissible, but not encouraged, for it to return the same thing as <code>toString</code>.</p>
        <p>The meanings of the optional parameters to this method are defined in the ECMA-402 specification; implementations that do not include ECMA-402 support must not use those parameter positions for anything else.</p>

114:T67c,<emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The behaviour of the functions <code>acos</code>, <code>acosh</code>, <code>asin</code>, <code>asinh</code>, <code>atan</code>, <code>atanh</code>, <code>atan2</code>, <code>cbrt</code>, <code>cos</code>, <code>cosh</code>, <code>exp</code>, <code>expm1</code>, <code>hypot</code>, <code>log</code>, <code>log1p</code>, <code>log2</code>, <code>log10</code>, <code>pow</code>, <code>random</code>, <code>sin</code>, <code>sinh</code>, <code>tan</code>, and <code>tanh</code> is not precisely specified here except to require specific results for certain argument values that represent boundary cases of interest. For other argument values, these functions are intended to compute approximations to the results of familiar mathematical functions, but some latitude is allowed in the choice of approximation algorithms. The general intent is that an implementer should be able to use the same mathematical library for ECMAScript on a given hardware platform that is available to C programmers on that platform.</p>
        <p>Although the choice of algorithms is left to the implementation, it is recommended (but not specified by this standard) that implementations use the approximation algorithms for <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography">IEEE 754-2019</a></emu-xref> arithmetic contained in <code>fdlibm</code>, the freely distributable mathematical library from Sun Microsystems (<a href="http://www.netlib.org/fdlibm">http://www.netlib.org/fdlibm</a>).</p>
      </div></emu-note>

<var>minute</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 59), <var>second</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 59), <var>millisecond</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref <var>microsecond</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref <var>nanosecond</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 999) and returns a BigInt. The returned value represents a number of nanoseconds since the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch">epoch</a></emu-xref> that corresponds to the given ISO 8601 calendar date and wall-clock time in UTC. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>date</var> be <emu-xref aoid="MakeDay"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-makeday">MakeDay</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>year</var>), <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>month</var> - 1), <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>day</var>)).</li><li>Let <var>time</var> be <emu-xref aoid="MakeTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-maketime">MakeTime</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>hour</var>), <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>minute</var>), <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>second</var>), <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>millisecond</var>)).</li><li>Let <var>ms</var> be <emu-xref aoid="MakeDate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-makedate">MakeDate</a></emu-xref>(<var>date</var>, <var>time</var>).</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>ms</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="ℤ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℤ">ℤ</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>ms</var>) × 10<sup>6</sup> + <var>microsecond</var> × 10<sup>3</sup> + <var>nanosecond</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>

149:Td99,<p>
          Time zones in ECMAScript are represented by <dfn variants="time zone identifier">time zone identifiers</dfn>, which are Strings composed entirely of code units in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0x0000 to 0x007F.
          Time zones supported by an ECMAScript implementation may be <dfn variants="available named time zone">available named time zones</dfn>, represented by the <var class="field">[[Identifier]]</var> field of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifier-record"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifier-record">Time Zone Identifier Records</a></emu-xref> returned by <emu-xref aoid="AvailableNamedTimeZoneIdentifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-availablenamedtimezoneidentifiers">AvailableNamedTimeZoneIdentifiers</a></emu-xref>, or <dfn variants="offset time zone">offset time zones</dfn>, represented by Strings for which <emu-xref aoid="IsTimeZoneOffsetString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-istimezoneoffsetstring">IsTimeZoneOffsetString</a></emu-xref> returns <emu-val>true</emu-val>.
        </p>
        <p>
          A <dfn variants="primary time zone identifiers">primary time zone identifier</dfn> is the preferred identifier for an available named time zone.
          A <dfn variants="non-primary time zone identifiers">non-primary time zone identifier</dfn> is an identifier for an available named time zone that is not a primary time zone identifier.
          An <dfn variants="available named time zone identifiers">available named time zone identifier</dfn> is either a primary time zone identifier or a non-primary time zone identifier.
          Each available named time zone identifier is associated with exactly one available named time zone.
          Each available named time zone is associated with exactly one primary time zone identifier and zero or more non-primary time zone identifiers.
        </p>
        <p>
          ECMAScript implementations must support an available named time zone with the identifier <emu-val>"UTC"</emu-val>, which must be the primary time zone identifier for the UTC time zone.
          In addition, implementations may support any number of other available named time zones.
        </p>
        <p>
          Implementations that follow the requirements for time zones as described in the ECMA-402 Internationalization API specification are called <dfn>time zone aware</dfn>.
          Time zone aware implementations must support available named time zones corresponding to the Zone and Link names of the IANA Time Zone Database, and only such names.
          In time zone aware implementations, a primary time zone identifier is a Zone name, and a non-primary time zone identifier is a Link name, respectively, in the IANA Time Zone Database except as specifically overridden by <emu-xref aoid="AvailableNamedTimeZoneIdentifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-availablenamedtimezoneidentifiers">AvailableNamedTimeZoneIdentifiers</a></emu-xref> as specified in the ECMA-402 specification.
          Implementations that do not support the entire IANA Time Zone Database are still recommended to use IANA Time Zone Database names as identifiers to represent time zones.
        </p>

14a:T2070,<p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> abstract operation GetNamedTimeZoneEpochNanoseconds takes arguments <var>timeZoneIdentifier</var> (a String), <var>year</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 1 to 31), <var>hour</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 23), <var>minute</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 59), <var>second</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 59), <var>millisecond</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 999), <var>microsecond</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 999), and <var>nanosecond</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 999) and returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of BigInts. Each value in the returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> represents a number of nanoseconds since the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#epoch">epoch</a></emu-xref> that corresponds to the given ISO 8601 calendar date and wall-clock time in the named time zone identified by <var>timeZoneIdentifier</var>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <p>
          When the input represents a local time occurring more than once because of a negative time zone transition (e.g. when daylight saving time ends or the time zone offset is decreased due to a time zone rule change), the returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> will have more than one element and will be sorted by ascending numerical value.
          When the input represents a local time skipped because of a positive time zone transition (e.g. when daylight saving time begins or the time zone offset is increased due to a time zone rule change), the returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> will be empty.
          Otherwise, the returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> will have one element.
        </p>
        <p>The default implementation of GetNamedTimeZoneEpochNanoseconds, to be used for ECMAScript implementations that do not include local political rules for any time zones, performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>timeZoneIdentifier</var> is <emu-val>"UTC"</emu-val>.</li><li>Let <var>epochNanoseconds</var> be <emu-xref aoid="GetUTCEpochNanoseconds"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-getutcepochnanoseconds">GetUTCEpochNanoseconds</a></emu-xref>(<var>year</var>, <var>month</var>, <var>day</var>, <var>hour</var>, <var>minute</var>, <var>second</var>, <var>millisecond</var>, <var>microsecond</var>, <var>nanosecond</var>).</li><li class="exit">Return « <var>epochNanoseconds</var> ».</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>It is required for <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">time zone aware</a></emu-xref> implementations (and recommended for all others) to use the time zone information of the IANA Time Zone Database <a href="https://www.iana.org/time-zones/">https://www.iana.org/time-zones/</a>.</p>
          <p>1:30 AM on 5 November 2017 in America/New_York is repeated twice, so GetNamedTimeZoneEpochNanoseconds(<emu-val>"America/New_York"</emu-val>, 2017, 11, 5, 1, 30, 0, 0, 0, 0) would return a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of length 2 in which the first element represents 05:30 UTC (corresponding with 01:30 US Eastern Daylight Time at UTC offset -04:00) and the second element represents 06:30 UTC (corresponding with 01:30 US Eastern Standard Time at UTC offset -05:00).</p>
          <p>2:30 AM on 12 March 2017 in America/New_York does not exist, so GetNamedTimeZoneEpochNanoseconds(<emu-val>"America/New_York"</emu-val>, 2017, 3, 12, 2, 30, 0, 0, 0, 0) would return an empty <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>.</p>

14c:Ta73,<p>A <dfn variants="Time Zone Identifier Records">Time Zone Identifier Record</dfn> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> used to describe an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">available named time zone identifier</a></emu-xref> and its corresponding <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">primary time zone identifier</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>Time Zone Identifier Records have the fields listed in <emu-xref href="#table-time-zone-identifier-record-fields"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#table-time-zone-identifier-record-fields">Table 59</a></emu-xref>.</p>

14e:Tcb0,<p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> abstract operation SystemTimeZoneIdentifier takes no arguments and returns a String. It returns a String representing the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref>'s current time zone, which is either a String representing a UTC offset for which <emu-xref aoid="IsTimeZoneOffsetString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-istimezoneoffsetstring">IsTimeZoneOffsetString</a></emu-xref> returns <emu-val>true</emu-val>, or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">primary time zone identifier</a></emu-xref>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>

        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If the implementation only supports the UTC time zone, return <emu-val>"UTC"</emu-val>.</li><li>Let <var>systemTimeZoneString</var> be the String representing the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref>'s current time zone, either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">primary time zone identifier</a></emu-xref> or an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">offset time zone</a></emu-xref> identifier.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>systemTimeZoneString</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>

        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>
            To ensure the level of functionality that implementations commonly provide in the methods of the Date object, it is recommended that SystemTimeZoneIdentifier return an IANA time zone name corresponding to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref>'s time zone setting, if such a thing exists.
            <emu-xref aoid="GetNamedTimeZoneEpochNanoseconds"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-getnamedtimezoneepochnanoseconds">GetNamedTimeZoneEpochNanoseconds</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref aoid="GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-getnamedtimezoneoffsetnanoseconds">GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds</a></emu-xref> must reflect the local political rules for standard time and daylight saving time in that time zone, if such rules exist.
          </p>
          <p>For example, if the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref> is a browser on a system where the user has chosen US Eastern Time as their time zone, SystemTimeZoneIdentifier returns <emu-val>"America/New_York"</emu-val>.</p>

14f:T1337,<p>The abstract operation LocalTime takes argument <var>tv</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref>) and returns an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref>. It converts <var>tv</var> from UTC to local time.
            The local political rules for standard time and daylight saving time in effect at <var>tv</var> should be used to determine the result in the way specified in this section. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>systemTimeZoneIdentifier</var> be <emu-xref aoid="SystemTimeZoneIdentifier"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-systemtimezoneidentifier">SystemTimeZoneIdentifier</a></emu-xref>().</li><li>If <emu-xref aoid="IsTimeZoneOffsetString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-istimezoneoffsetstring">IsTimeZoneOffsetString</a></emu-xref>(<var>systemTimeZoneIdentifier</var>) is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>offsetNs</var> be <emu-xref aoid="ParseTimeZoneOffsetString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-parsetimezoneoffsetstring">ParseTimeZoneOffsetString</a></emu-xref>(<var>systemTimeZoneIdentifier</var>).</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li>Let <var>offsetNs</var> be <emu-xref aoid="GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-getnamedtimezoneoffsetnanoseconds">GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds</a></emu-xref>(<var>systemTimeZoneIdentifier</var>, <emu-xref aoid="ℤ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℤ">ℤ</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>tv</var>) × 10<sup>6</sup>)).</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>offsetMs</var> be <emu-xref aoid="truncate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#eqn-truncate">truncate</a></emu-xref>(<var>offsetNs</var> / 10<sup>6</sup>).</li><li class="exit">Return <var>tv</var> + <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>offsetMs</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>If political rules for the local time <var>tv</var> are not available within the implementation, the result is <var>tv</var> because <emu-xref aoid="SystemTimeZoneIdentifier"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-systemtimezoneidentifier">SystemTimeZoneIdentifier</a></emu-xref> returns <emu-val>"UTC"</emu-val> and <emu-xref aoid="GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-getnamedtimezoneoffsetnanoseconds">GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds</a></emu-xref> returns 0.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>It is required for <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">time zone aware</a></emu-xref> implementations (and recommended for all others) to use the time zone information of the IANA Time Zone Database <a href="https://www.iana.org/time-zones/">https://www.iana.org/time-zones/</a>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Two different input <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time values</a></emu-xref> <emu-eqn class="inline"><var>tv</var><sub>UTC</sub></emu-eqn> are converted to the same local time <emu-eqn class="inline">t<sub>local</sub></emu-eqn> at a negative time zone transition when there are repeated times (e.g. the daylight saving time ends or the time zone adjustment is decreased.).</p>
          <p><emu-eqn class="inline">LocalTime(<emu-xref aoid="UTC"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-utc-t">UTC</a></emu-xref>(<var>tv</var><sub>local</sub>))</emu-eqn> is not necessarily always equal to <emu-eqn class="inline"><var>tv</var><sub>local</sub></emu-eqn>. Correspondingly, <emu-eqn class="inline"><emu-xref aoid="UTC"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-utc-t">UTC</a></emu-xref>(LocalTime(<var>tv</var><sub>UTC</sub>))</emu-eqn> is not necessarily always equal to <emu-eqn class="inline"><var>tv</var><sub>UTC</sub></emu-eqn>.</p>

href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-datefromtime">DateFromTime</a></emu-xref>(<var>tBefore</var>)), <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="HourFromTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-hourfromtime">HourFromTime</a></emu-xref>(<var>tBefore</var>)), <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="MinFromTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-minfromtime">MinFromTime</a></emu-xref>(<var>tBefore</var>)), <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="SecFromTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-secfromtime">SecFromTime</a></emu-xref>(<var>tBefore</var>)), <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="msFromTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-msfromtime">msFromTime</a></emu-xref>(<var>tBefore</var>)), 0, 0), where <var>tBefore</var> is the largest <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref> &lt; <var>t</var> for which <var>possibleInstantsBefore</var> is not empty (i.e., <var>tBefore</var> represents the last local time before the transition).</li><li>Let <var>disambiguatedInstant</var> be the last element of <var>possibleInstantsBefore</var>.</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>offsetNs</var> be <emu-xref aoid="GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-getnamedtimezoneoffsetnanoseconds">GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds</a></emu-xref>(<var>systemTimeZoneIdentifier</var>, <var>disambiguatedInstant</var>).</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>offsetMs</var> be <emu-xref aoid="truncate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#eqn-truncate">truncate</a></emu-xref>(<var>offsetNs</var> / 10<sup>6</sup>).</li><li class="exit">Return <var>t</var> - <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>offsetMs</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <p>
          Input <var>t</var> is nominally a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref> but may be any Number value.
          The algorithm must not limit <var>t</var> to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref> range, so that inputs corresponding with a boundary of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref> range can be supported regardless of local UTC offset.
          For example, the maximum <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref> is 8.64 × 10<sup>15</sup>, corresponding with <emu-val>"+275760-09-13T00:00:00Z"</emu-val>.
          In an environment where the local time zone offset is ahead of UTC by 1 hour at that instant, it is represented by the larger input of 8.64 × 10<sup>15</sup> + 3.6 × 10<sup>6</sup>, corresponding with <emu-val>"+275760-09-13T01:00:00+01:00"</emu-val>.
        </p>
        <p>If political rules for the local time <var>t</var> are not available within the implementation, the result is <var>t</var> because <emu-xref aoid="SystemTimeZoneIdentifier"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-systemtimezoneidentifier">SystemTimeZoneIdentifier</a></emu-xref> returns <emu-val>"UTC"</emu-val> and <emu-xref aoid="GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-getnamedtimezoneoffsetnanoseconds">GetNamedTimeZoneOffsetNanoseconds</a></emu-xref> returns 0.</p>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>It is required for <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-identifiers">time zone aware</a></emu-xref> implementations (and recommended for all others) to use the time zone information of the IANA Time Zone Database <a href="https://www.iana.org/time-zones/">https://www.iana.org/time-zones/</a>.</p>
          <p>
            1:30 AM on 5 November 2017 in America/New_York is repeated twice (fall backward), but it must be interpreted as 1:30 AM UTC-04 instead of 1:30 AM UTC-05.
            In UTC(<emu-xref aoid="TimeClip"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-timeclip">TimeClip</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="MakeDate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-makedate">MakeDate</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="MakeDay"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-makeday">MakeDay</a></emu-xref>(2017, 10, 5), <emu-xref aoid="MakeTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-maketime">MakeTime</a></emu-xref>(1, 30, 0, 0)))), the value of <var>offsetMs</var> is <emu-eqn class="inline">-4 × <emu-xref aoid="msPerHour"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#eqn-msPerHour">msPerHour</a></emu-xref></emu-eqn>.
          </p>
          <p>
            2:30 AM on 12 March 2017 in America/New_York does not exist, but it must be interpreted as 2:30 AM UTC-05 (equivalent to 3:30 AM UTC-04).
            In UTC(<emu-xref aoid="TimeClip"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-timeclip">TimeClip</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="MakeDate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-makedate">MakeDate</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="MakeDay"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-makeday">MakeDay</a></emu-xref>(2017, 2, 12), <emu-xref aoid="MakeTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-maketime">MakeTime</a></emu-xref>(2, 30, 0, 0)))), the value of <var>offsetMs</var> is <emu-eqn class="inline">-5 × <emu-xref aoid="msPerHour"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#eqn-msPerHour">msPerHour</a></emu-xref></emu-eqn>.
          </p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p><emu-eqn class="inline">UTC(<emu-xref aoid="LocalTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-localtime">LocalTime</a></emu-xref>(<var>t</var><sub>UTC</sub>))</emu-eqn> is not necessarily always equal to <emu-eqn class="inline"><var>t</var><sub>UTC</sub></emu-eqn>. Correspondingly, <emu-eqn class="inline"><emu-xref aoid="LocalTime"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-localtime">LocalTime</a></emu-xref>(UTC(<var>t</var><sub>local</sub>))</emu-eqn> is not necessarily always equal to <emu-eqn class="inline"><var>t</var><sub>local</sub></emu-eqn>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

155:T4e8,<p>The abstract operation TimeClip takes argument <var>time</var> (a Number) and returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref>. It calculates a number of milliseconds. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If <var>time</var> is not <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <emu-xref aoid="abs"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#eqn-abs">abs</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>time</var>)) > 8.64 × 10<sup>15</sup>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(! <emu-xref aoid="ToIntegerOrInfinity"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tointegerorinfinity">ToIntegerOrInfinity</a></emu-xref>(<var>time</var>)).</li></ol></emu-alg>
      fc:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-timeclip","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"214131-timeclip--time-","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"21.4.1.31"}]," TimeClip ( ",["$","var",null,{"children":"time"}]," )"],["$","$Laf",null,{"id":"214131-timeclip--time-"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$155"}}]]}]
156:T15b3,<p>ECMAScript defines a string interchange format for date-times based upon a simplification of the ISO 8601 calendar date extended format. The format is as follows: <code>YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.sssZ</code></p>
        <p>Where the elements are as follows:</p>
        <figure>
          <table class="lightweight-table">
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>YYYY</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the year in the proleptic Gregorian calendar as four decimal digits from 0000 to 9999, or as an <emu-xref href="#sec-expanded-years"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-expanded-years">expanded year</a></emu-xref> of <emu-val>"+"</emu-val> or <emu-val>"-"</emu-val> followed by six decimal digits.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>-</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                <emu-val>"-"</emu-val> (hyphen) appears literally twice in the string.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>MM</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the month of the year as two decimal digits from 01 (January) to 12 (December).
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>DD</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the day of the month as two decimal digits from 01 to 31.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>T</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                <emu-val>"T"</emu-val> appears literally in the string, to indicate the beginning of the time element.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>HH</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the number of complete hours that have passed since midnight as two decimal digits from 00 to 24.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>:</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                <emu-val>":"</emu-val> (colon) appears literally twice in the string.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>mm</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the number of complete minutes since the start of the hour as two decimal digits from 00 to 59.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>ss</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the number of complete seconds since the start of the minute as two decimal digits from 00 to 59.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>.</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                <emu-val>"."</emu-val> (dot) appears literally in the string.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>sss</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the number of complete milliseconds since the start of the second as three decimal digits.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <code>Z</code>
              </td>
              <td>
                is the UTC offset representation specified as <emu-val>"Z"</emu-val> (for UTC with no offset) or as either <emu-val>"+"</emu-val> or <emu-val>"-"</emu-val> followed by a time expression <code>HH:mm</code> (a subset of the <emu-xref href="#sec-time-zone-offset-strings"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-zone-offset-strings">time zone offset string format</a></emu-xref> for indicating local time ahead of or behind UTC, respectively)
              </td>
            </tr>
          </table>
        </figure>
        <p>This format includes date-only forms:</p>
        <pre>
YYYY
YYYY-MM
YYYY-MM-DD
        </pre>
        <p>It also includes “date-time” forms that consist of one of the above date-only forms immediately followed by one of the following time forms with an optional UTC offset representation appended:</p>
        <pre>
THH:mm
THH:mm:ss
THH:mm:ss.sss
        </pre>
        <p>A string containing out-of-bounds or nonconforming elements is not a valid instance of this format.</p>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>As every day both starts and ends with midnight, the two notations <code>00:00</code> and <code>24:00</code> are available to distinguish the two midnights that can be associated with one date. This means that the following two notations refer to exactly the same point in time: <code>1995-02-04T24:00</code> and <code>1995-02-05T00:00</code>. This interpretation of the latter form as “end of a calendar day” is consistent with ISO 8601, even though that specification reserves it for describing time intervals and does not permit it within representations of single points in time.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>There exists no international standard that specifies abbreviations for civil time zones like CET, EST, etc. and sometimes the same abbreviation is even used for two very different time zones. For this reason, both ISO 8601 and this format specify numeric representations of time zone offsets.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

158:T300e,<p>
          ECMAScript defines a string interchange format for UTC offsets, derived from ISO 8601.
          The format is described by the following grammar.
        </p>

        fe:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-time-zone-offset-strings","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"214133-time-zone-offset-string-format","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"21.4.1.33"}]," Time Zone Offset String Format"],["$","$Laf",null,{"id":"214133-time-zone-offset-string-format"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$158"}}],"$L159","$L15a"]}]
15b:T766,<p>The Date <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref>:</p>
      <ul>
        <li>is <dfn><emu-intrinsic>%Date%</emu-intrinsic></dfn>.</li>
        <li>is the initial value of the <emu-val>"Date"</emu-val> property of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object">global object</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        <li>creates and initializes a new Date when called as a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        <li>returns a String representing the current time (UTC) when called as a function rather than as a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        <li>is a function whose behaviour differs based upon the number and types of its arguments.</li>
        <li>may be used as the value of an <code>extends</code> clause of a class definition.       </ul>

15d:T114b,<p>This function applies the <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref> operator to its argument. If <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref> results in an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">abrupt completion</a></emu-xref> the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref> is immediately returned. Otherwise, this function interprets the resulting String as a date and time; it returns a Number, the UTC <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref> corresponding to the date and time. The String may be interpreted as a local time, a UTC time, or a time in some other time zone, depending on the contents of the String. The function first attempts to parse the String according to the format described in Date Time String Format (<emu-xref href="#sec-date-time-string-format"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-date-time-string-format">21.4.1.32</a></emu-xref>), including expanded years. If the String does not conform to that format the function may fall back to any implementation-specific heuristics or implementation-specific date formats. Strings that are unrecognizable or contain out-of-bounds format element values shall cause this function to return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</p>
        <p>If the String conforms to the <emu-xref href="#sec-date-time-string-format"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-date-time-string-format">Date Time String Format</a></emu-xref>, substitute values take the place of absent format elements. When the <code>MM</code> or <code>DD</code> elements are absent, <emu-val>"01"</emu-val> is used. When the <code>HH</code>, <code>mm</code>, or <code>ss</code> elements are absent, <emu-val>"00"</emu-val> is used. When the <code>sss</code> element is absent, <emu-val>"000"</emu-val> is used. When the UTC offset representation is absent, date-only forms are interpreted as a UTC time and date-time forms are interpreted as a local time.</p>
        <p>If <code>x</code> is any Date whose milliseconds amount is zero within a particular implementation of ECMAScript, then all of the following expressions should produce the same numeric value in that implementation, if all the properties referenced have their initial values:</p>
        <pre><code class="hljs javascript">
          x.<span class="hljs-title function_">valueOf</span>()
          <span class="hljs-title class_">Date</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">parse</span>(x.<span class="hljs-title function_">toString</span>())
          <span class="hljs-title class_">Date</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">parse</span>(x.<span class="hljs-title function_">toUTCString</span>())
          <span class="hljs-title class_">Date</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">parse</span>(x.<span class="hljs-title function_">toISOString</span>())
        </code></pre>
        <p>However, the expression</p>
        <pre><code class="hljs javascript">
          <span class="hljs-title class_">Date</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">parse</span>(x.<span class="hljs-title function_">toLocaleString</span>())
        </code></pre>
        <p>is not required to produce the same Number value as the preceding three expressions and, in general, the value produced by this function is <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> when given any String value that does not conform to the Date Time String Format (<emu-xref href="#sec-date-time-string-format"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-date-time-string-format">21.4.1.32</a></emu-xref>) and that could not be produced in that implementation by the <code>toString</code> or <code>toUTCString</code> method.</p>

        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This operation is not the same as casting to binary32 and then to binary16 because of the possibility of double-rounding: consider the number <var>k</var> = <emu-val>1.00048828125000022204</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, for example, for which Math.f16round(<var>k</var>) is <emu-val>1.0009765625</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, but Math.f16round(Math.fround(<var>k</var>)) is <emu-val>1</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</p>
          <p>Not all platforms provide native support for casting from binary64 to binary16. There are various libraries which can provide this, including the MIT-licensed <a href="https://half.sourceforge.net/">half</a> library. Alternatively, it is possible to first cast from binary64 to binary32 under roundTiesToEven and then check whether the result could lead to incorrect double-rounding. Such cases can be handled explicitly by adjusting the mantissa of the binary32 value so that it is the value which would be produced by performing the initial cast under roundTiesToOdd. Casting the adjusted value to binary16 under roundTiesToEven then produces the correct value.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

1a6:Tad3,<p>This function returns the Number value that is closest to <var>x</var> and is integral. If two <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Numbers</a></emu-xref> are equally close to <var>x</var>, then the result is the Number value that is closer to +∞.         <p>It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>n</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToNumber"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tonumber">ToNumber</a></emu-xref>(<var>x</var>).</li><li class="exit">If <var>n</var> is not <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> or <var>n</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref>, return <var>n</var>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>n</var> &lt; <emu-val>0.5</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> and <var>n</var> > <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>n</var> &lt; <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> and <var>n</var> ≥ <emu-val>-0.5</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li><li class="exit">Return the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref> closest to <var>n</var>, preferring the Number closer to +∞ in the case of a tie.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">

1ad:Tb36,<p>Covering the full <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref> range of approximately 273,790 years forward or backward from 1 January 1970 (<emu-xref href="#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">21.4.1.1</a></emu-xref>) requires representing years before 0 or after 9999. ISO 8601 permits expansion of the year representation, but only by mutual agreement of the partners in information interchange. In the simplified ECMAScript format, such an expanded year representation shall have 6 digits and is always prefixed with a + or - sign. The year 0 is considered positive and must be prefixed with a + sign. The representation of the year 0 as -000000 is invalid. Strings matching the <emu-xref href="#sec-date-time-string-format"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-date-time-string-format">Date Time String Format</a></emu-xref> with expanded years representing instants in time outside the range of a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-time-values-and-time-range">time value</a></emu-xref> are treated as unrecognizable by <emu-xref href="#sec-date.parse"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-date.parse"><code>Date.parse</code></a></emu-xref> and cause that function to return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> without falling back to implementation-specific behaviour or heuristics.</p>
          <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>Examples of date-<emu-not-ref>time values</emu-not-ref> with expanded years:</p>
            <figure>
              <table class="lightweight-table">
                <tr>
                  <td>-271821-04-20T00:00:00Z</td>
                  <td>271822 B.C.</td>
                </tr>
                <tr>
                  <td>-000001-01-01T00:00:00Z</td>
                  <td>2 B.C.</td>
                </tr>
                <tr>
                  <td>+000000-01-01T00:00:00Z</td>
                  <td>1 B.C.</td>
                </tr>
                <tr>
                  <td>+000001-01-01T00:00:00Z</td>
                  <td>1 A.D.</td>
                </tr>
                <tr>
                  <td>+001970-01-01T00:00:00Z</td>
                  <td>1970 A.D.</td>
                </tr>
                <tr>
                  <td>+002009-12-15T00:00:00Z</td>
                  <td>2009 A.D.</td>
                </tr>
                <tr>
                  <td>+275760-09-13T00:00:00Z</td>
                  <td>275760 A.D.</td>
                </tr>
              </table>
            </figure>
